# Formalization progress register

[Progress hub](README.md) · [Route diagrams](README.md#proof-progress-tree)

**403 local Blueprint nodes · 36 registered Lean certificates.**

Generated from the canonical records of the Git index used for this run, identified
by the canonical input digest below. These are recorded proof/definition statuses,
not a fresh Lean run or a remote CI receipt. A missing local proof means only that this
register does not certify it; unregistered auxiliary progress appears in the diagram directory below.

![✓ Formalized in Lean](badges/formalized.svg) · ![○ Not yet formalized](badges/pending.svg) · ![◐ Partially formalized](badges/partial.svg) · ![◇ Open hypothesis, not a target](badges/hypothesis.svg) · ![○ Not formalized, superseded route](badges/superseded.svg) · ![— Excluded: refuted](badges/excluded.svg)

Open hypotheses remain open mathematically; they are assumptions or questions, not current formalization targets. Superseded results and route junctions belong to historical exploratory routes. KLS is proved through the accepted BKL main line. The four exploratory routes are not current formalization targets and are retained as historical records; their unproved hypotheses remain open mathematically.

Upstream status is shown only where an explicit upstream mapping exists; no mapping is inferred
from a similar title. Upstream `proved` does not mean Lean-proved.

The source premise E d_i f = 0 is dropped (a finite dual norm forces it). It adds: for EVERY real symmetric B, tr(B H B H) is nu-integrable and int tr(B H B H) dnu <= 2 tr(B B) (Theorem 2.5 with the exact constant 2, weak C^{1,1} form for this witness); the weighted integration by parts int D_v eta dnu = int eta D_v u dnu for every bounded globally Lipschitz eta; and the coordinate source Stein identity for every C1, bounded and globally Lipschitz test. This is not the smooth moment map of Lemma 2.2, not a constructed Stein kernel, and not Theorem 1.2 or the constant-8 bound. | Local classification: local_argument | ![✓ Formalized in Lean](badges/formalized.svg) | [Statement](../../formalization/blueprint/src/chapters/45-shared-analytic-bridges.tex#L4965) |

Not the source moment map, A.6 for the source law, Theorem 2.5 or 1.2. | Local Lean result; no upstream mapping | ![✓ Formalized in Lean](badges/formalized.svg) | [Statement](../../formalization/blueprint/src/chapters/45-shared-analytic-bridges.tex#L2472) |
| `thm:letwin-weak-hessian-trace-bound` | This is the weak C^{1,1} form of Letwin Theorem 2.5 for a given potential, with positive semidefinite B. Let nu = e^{-U} dx with U convex, C1, globally Lipschitz, with Lipschitz gradient, int e^{-U} = 1 and almost everywhere positive definite Hessian; let T be a weak Hessian tensor in L2 on every compact set, and let the inverse Hessian entries be essentially bounded on every compact set. Assume as hypotheses that the Hessian entries are nu-integrable with mean Id, that D = sum_kl (Hess U)^-1_kl tr(B T_k B T_l) is nu-integrable and that the half-energy bound int D dnu <= (1/2) int tr(B Hess U B Hess U) dnu holds. Then tr(B Hess U B Hess U) is nu-integrable and its integral is at most 2 tr(B B). | `thm:logconcave-cheeger-poincare-comparison` | Prove both numerical comparisons for every finite-dimensional log-concave probability law: C_P^lit(mu) <= 4 Psi_KLS(mu)^2 and Psi_KLS(mu)^2 <= pi C_P^lit(mu). The exact pi Buser half now passes from actual smooth Gaussian convolutions through a finite Cheeger test bound under setwise convergence, Poincare upper stability, and intrinsic-coordinate transport. Zero-dimensional affine support and extended optima are included. Dependencies below are statement dependencies, not a claim that each dependency is open.
Open hypotheses remain open mathematically and are not current formalization targets.
The four exploratory routes are not current formalization targets and are retained as
historical records; their unproved hypotheses remain open mathematically.
KLS is proved through the accepted BKL main line.
Superseded-route dependencies and gaps are retained for historical context.
Infrastructure gaps are the recorded assessment and may cover a broader parent task.

| `BV` | Metric BV perimeter and half-mass Cheeger interfaces on the actual closed intrinsic support are defined. Complement symmetry is formalized for finite Borel measures on metric spaces, including the closed intrinsic support law; the zero-dimensional convention and an infinite-measure counterexample are proved. The half-mass and symmetric BV Cheeger constants are proved equal on every probability metric space. For a finite measure, globally Lipschitz approximants converging in global L1 may replace bounded-support tests and bound the perimeter, and on a proper metric space so may locally Lipschitz approximants (even under L1 convergence on every closed ball only, through clamping), the relaxation infima over both classes being equal to the perimeter (finite measures; the locally Lipschitz class on proper spaces with global or compact-set L1 convergence, in particular on the closed intrinsic support of every finite-dimensional law); epsilon recovery tests exist below every threshold above the perimeter, and every finite perimeter is the limit of the costs of an explicitly selected approximating sequence (for any Borel measure; nothing is claimed for infinite perimeter); the perimeter is sequentially lower semicontinuous under global L1 convergence of measurable set indicators for every Borel measure; for every probability law on a metric space it is at most the outer Minkowski content of every measurable set (one inequality, via two-radius plateau ramps), and the Minkowski coarea inequality holds with the actual local slope for every globally Lipschitz function, bounding the integrated superlevel content and BV perimeter of every test by its cost; every test has a quantitative superlevel selection with content at most cost/(1-2 delta) + epsilon and indicator error at most its L1 error over delta. For every set of finite BV perimeter, explicit measurable superlevel sets converge to it in L1 with Minkowski contents converging to its perimeter (Minkowski recovery), and the relaxed lower Minkowski content, the infimum of liminf outer contents over measurable sets converging in L1, equals the perimeter for every set; for measurable sets of mass strictly between 0 and 1 the recovery masses converge, eventually stay in (0, 1), and the symmetric Cheeger quotients converge to the BV quotient. The symmetric and half-mass BV Cheeger constants equal the Minkowski Cheeger constant for every probability law on a normed space, and for the intrinsic coordinate law they equal the isoperimetric constant (an equality of infima; the pointwise reverse inequality is false in general). Restriction to the closed measure support does not increase the metric BV perimeter of the trace of a set (one inequality, via restricted tests and the isometric-inclusion slope bound), with equality under an actual nonexpansive retraction onto a full-mass measurable subset, in particular, via the nearest-point projection, for every probability law on a Hilbert space with convex closed support, where the half-mass and symmetric BV Cheeger constants of the law and of its closed-support law agree. For every finite-dimensional log-concave probability law, the BV Cheeger constant on the closed intrinsic support therefore equals the isoperimetric constant. The upper Minkowski content, the limsup of the same radius quotient, is at least the lower content; for every set the real mass of its open s-thickening is monotone, hence differentiable at Lebesgue-almost every radius, at every differentiability radius the upper content of that thickening is at most the derivative, and the upper content of the s-thickening integrated over s in (0, r) is at most the mass increment nu(B^r) - nu(B) for every set and every radius (probability laws on normed groups; inequalities only, with no measurability of the content, integrability of the derivative or absolute continuity of the radius mass assumed). Every measurable set of finite lower content is recovered by thickenings converging in L1 with limsup of upper contents at most its lower content; every set of finite BV perimeter is recovered by measurable sets converging in L1 whose upper contents tend to its perimeter; and the relaxed upper Minkowski content, the infimum of liminf upper contents over the same measurable approximating class, equals the perimeter for every set, hence the relaxed lower content (equalities of infima; the pointwise upper content is not identified with the perimeter). This content chain (both contents, the local-slope Minkowski coarea inequality, the bridge, both recoveries and both relaxed identities) holds on arbitrary metric spaces with a Borel probability law, the generic coarea using the lower semicontinuous open-ball supremum; since the evening of 2026-09-30 the chain holds for every finite Borel measure (scaling by nonnegative real scalars, the zero measure, normalization of a finite nonzero measure, both recoveries and both relaxed identities at finite total mass, arbitrary targets). This ✓ covers exactly that log-concave (convex-support) identification for the bounded-support relaxation; The locally Lipschitz classes on nonproper spaces, nonconvex support transfer, general metric-measure/RCD comparisons remain open; KLS is proved through the accepted BKL main line. A supporting test-cost estimate uses closed function support mass rather than total mass: every bounded-support test has finite cost under a measure finite on bounded Borel sets, including infinite total mass. The actual globally Lipschitz relaxation with global L1 convergence equals the bounded-support perimeter for every measurable finite-mass target under any Borel measure on a metric space, using cutoff bounds by the original indicator error and target-tail mass; this bounded-support reduction neither requires properness nor identifies a Minkowski relaxation. The direct positive-superlevel local-slope coarea bound holds when a positive-support neighborhood has finite measure, and its all-level version holds for nonnegative functions; restriction to that neighborhood preserves lower content at positive superlevels. For any Borel measure the perimeter/content bridge holds at measurable finite-mass targets. The full Lemma 3.2 chain is formalized with the actual right-radius liminf of integrated open-ball growth between positive-level content and original local-slope integrals, under nonnegativity and finite positive-support-neighborhood mass. Actual bounded measurable lower-recovery sets have finite mass and finite lower content term by term, converge in global indicator L1 and have contents converging to perimeter at measurable finite-mass targets under measures finite on bounded Borel sets. The existing relaxed lower-content infimum equals perimeter at that scope, including infinite perimeter; finite prefixes with infinite mass remain allowed because convergence ensures eventual finite mass. Finite target mass and finite lower content imply a finite-mass positive neighborhood. Radius-local restriction and a tail of small radii prove actual lower-to-upper recovery with original global indicator L1 convergence. Diagonal selection gives bounded open finite-mass upper-recovery sets with finite upper content and contents converging to finite perimeter. The existing relaxed upper infimum equals perimeter, hence the lower relaxation, at the published finite-mass Borel target scope, including infinite perimeter by order and infinite-mass finite prefixes. Lemma 2.1 also exposes relaxed upper content <= lower content at measurable finite-mass targets under any Borel measure. Nonconvex support transfer, locally Lipschitz classes on nonproper spaces and general RCD comparisons remain separate; no ledger promotion is claimed. Lemma 2.1's equality of the relaxed lower and upper Minkowski contents also holds for every Borel measure at measurable finite-mass targets (2026-10-01), and on proper metric spaces the Remark 3.5 locally Lipschitz relaxations with global or compact-set L1 convergence equal the bounded-support perimeter at measurable finite-mass targets for every Borel measure (2026-10-01). | ![✓ Formalized in Lean](badges/formalized.svg) | [Source](https://github.com/human-ai-mathematics/kls-lean/blob/main/formalization/FunctionalInequalities/KLS/Geometry/Cheeger/BVIdentification.lean) |

| `DECAY` | For the actual full-dimensional log-concave weighted-gradient heat semigroup, an explicit positive form-domain Poincare constant C implies centered L2 squared-norm decay exp(-2t/C) for every L2 datum and t >= 0. The closed unit-gradient form optimum equals the intrinsic literature Poincare constant; a positive finite literature upper bound now supplies the gap. The Markov indicator L1/correlation identity feeds the smooth-model pi Buser proof. Arbitrary nonsmooth-law pi passage remains open. | ![✓ Formalized in Lean](badges/formalized.svg) | [Source](https://github.com/human-ai-mathematics/kls-lean/blob/main/formalization/FunctionalInequalities/KLS/LogConcave/HeatSemigroup/SpectralGap/Decay.lean) |
| `GAUSS` | For every finite-dimensional real inner product space, including dimension zero, every Borell probability density and every positive inverse variance, the actual normalized Gaussian convolution density and its negative logarithm are C-infinity. Each finite Gaussian-kernel derivative is uniformly bounded, allowing iterated dominated differentiation under the source probability law without source moments. Poincare stability and the Cheeger-infimum passage are separately proved bridges; no individual-domain perimeter limit is claimed. | ![✓ Formalized in Lean](badges/formalized.svg) | [Source](https://github.com/human-ai-mathematics/kls-lean/blob/main/formalization/FunctionalInequalities/KLS/LogConcave/Density/GaussianSmoothing/Basic.lean) |
| `GSTAB` | For every Borell probability density on a finite-dimensional real inner product space, including dimension zero, the intrinsic literature Poincare optimum of its actual normalized Gaussian convolution is at most the source optimum plus 1/(2s) for s > 0. The proof identifies the Gaussian factor by its characteristic function and transports smooth-test bounds through product and convolution; it handles infinite optima without source moments or attainment assumptions. Together with the Cheeger test stability bridge, this yields general nonsmooth pi Buser; individual-domain perimeter convergence is not asserted. | ![✓ Formalized in Lean](badges/formalized.svg) | [Source](https://github.com/human-ai-mathematics/kls-lean/blob/main/formalization/FunctionalInequalities/KLS/LogConcave/Density/GaussianSmoothing/Poincare.lean) |

In the smooth convex tilted-law model, the kernel consists of constants; a positive Hessian lower bound yields the BGL Poincare estimate without an added ergodicity premise.
